Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm not wasting your time, I know you're probably very busy. But, well, I guess what brings me in is... I've just been waking up lately with this kind of heavy, hollow sadness. 

It's a little embarrassing to admit, honestly, because I usually try to stay so positive for my dancers. But it's almost like... like the studio lights are on, but there’s just no music in me anymore, if that makes sense? I'm a choreographer, so I guess that's just how my brain works. I'm sure it's nothing major, probably just a funk, but I wanted to make sure I'm doing the right thing by getting it checked out. Is that... is that okay to be here for?

description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: I feel wiped out after small things, like answering one email to a dancer or showering with this ankle brace on, and lying down doesn’t really recharge me.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep replaying the cancelled performances and telling myself I let the dancers, the funders, and the whole I hope I didn't wait too long to come see you about it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I guess I still enjoy it somewhat. I mean, I love my students, I really do. They're wonderful. But... maybe not quite as much as I used to? 

It's just that it feels a bit more like a chore now, which sounds terrible to say out loud. I feel so guilty even admitting that. I still show up, and I still put on a smile, and I really try to give them my best. I just... I don't get that same spark I used to get when a routine finally comes together. But I'm still doing it! I haven't quit or anything. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad teacher. Do a lot of people lose interest in their passions like that?

Well, I mean... I wouldn't say it's anything too extreme, but I guess I have been feeling a little more tired than usual? I know it's probably just because I'm not moving around as much with my ankle brace and everything. 

But sometimes, um, I just feel kind of wiped out after doing really small things. Like, I'll answer just one email to a dancer about scheduling, or I'll take a shower—which is a whole production with this brace, honestly—and I'll just feel completely exhausted afterward. And even if I go lie down for a bit, it doesn't really seem to recharge me the way it used to. I try not to let it show, and I still get up and do what I need to do! I'm not just lying around all day, I promise. It's just a little harder to find my energy right now. That's pretty normal when you're feeling down, isn't it? I'm sure it's nothing to worry about.

Like, if I just kept moving and kept smiling for the classes, it would eventually lift. It's really not that bad most days, just sort of... there in the background. I hope I didn't wait too long to come see you about it. Is nine months... is that typical for this kind of thing?\n\nDoctor: Did something specific happen in your life around the time this sadness started, about nine months ago?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I've racked my brain trying to figure that out, too. I kept thinking, maybe I was just stressed about a recital or maybe I wasn't eating right, but... no. Everything was actually going pretty well. The studio was doing great, my classes were full. \n\nI guess that's part of why I feel a little silly being here. Like, I shouldn't be sad, right? I have so much to be grateful for. I really hope it's okay that I don't have a better reason for feeling this way. Is it... is it normal for it to just kind of happen out of nowhere like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, such as choreographing or teaching dance classes, as much as you did before this sadness started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I still enjoy it somewhat. I mean, I love my students, I really do. They're wonderful. But... maybe not quite as much as I used to? \n\nIt's just that it feels a bit more like a chore now, which sounds terrible to say out loud. I feel so guilty even admitting that. I still show up, and I still put on a smile, and I really try to give them my best. I just... I don't get that same spark I used to get when a routine finally comes together. But I'm still doing it! I haven't quit or anything. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad teacher. Do a lot of people lose interest in their passions like that?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or things that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's hard to say right now. I mean, I still like going for walks when the weather is nice, and I try to read a little before bed. But... I don't know, nothing really stands out as being super exciting anymore. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably not a very helpful answer. I really wish I could give you a list of things I'm still super passionate about. I just feel so flat, you know? But I am trying! I promise I'm trying to stay active and do things. I really do try to keep up with my normal life. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing all day. I'm definitely still trying to participate in things. It's just that the joy isn't... quite as bright as it used to be? I hope that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since this sadness started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I'm sure we can fix it, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your energy levels over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I wouldn't say it's anything too extreme, but I guess I have been feeling a little more tired than usual? I know it's probably just because I'm not moving around as much with my ankle brace and everything. \n\nBut sometimes, um, I just feel kind of wiped out after doing really small things. Like, I'll answer just one email to a dancer about scheduling, or I'll take a shower—which is a whole production with this brace, honestly—and I'll just feel completely exhausted afterward. And even if I go lie down for a bit, it doesn't really seem to recharge me the way it used to. I try not to let it show, and I still get up and do what I need to do! I'm not just lying around all day, I promise. It's just a little harder to find my energy right now. That's pretty normal when you're feeling down, isn't it? Oh, um... two whole months? I don't... I don't think I've had a stretch quite that long where I felt completely a hundred percent.

Um... yeah, I guess I have. I really hate admitting that, because I feel like I should be stronger, you know? I want to be doing better for you. But... almost every single day, I've been waking up with this... this heavy, hollow sadness.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... sleep? I guess it's been a little bit uneven, maybe? I really hope that's normal. I mean, I try to get my eight hours, I really do, because I know how important rest is for my body. 

But... well, it's been a little all over the place. Like, there were those times when I just had so much energy, and I'd stay up sketching out new choreography formations until dawn. I felt so productive then, so I hope that's okay? But lately... lately it's different. I'll fall asleep because I'm just so exhausted, but then I keep waking up really early, like around four or five in the morning, and my stomach just feels... tight. Like I'm anxious, but I don't know why. 

I try to go back to sleep, I promise I do. I just lie there and try to relax. Is it bad that my sleep is so messed up? I really want to fix it if I'm doing something wrong.

Um... no, not really. I mean, I still love dance. I really do. It's my whole life, you know? I try so hard to stay engaged with my students and the choreography.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm not wasting your time, I know you're probably very busy. But, well, I guess what brings me in is... I've just been waking up lately with this kind of heavy, hollow sadness. \n\nIt's a little embarrassing to admit, honestly, because I usually try to stay so positive for my dancers. But it's almost like... like the studio lights are on, but there’s just no music in me anymore, if that makes sense? I'm a choreographer, so I guess that's just how my brain works. I'm sure it's nothing major, probably just a funk, but I wanted to make sure I'm doing the right thing by getting it checked out. Is that... is that okay to be here for?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this heavy, hollow sadness?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's been a little while. Maybe around nine months? \n\nI know that sounds like a long time to just let it sit, and I really should have come in sooner, I'm sorry. I just kept thinking I could push through it, you know? Like, if I just kept moving and kept smiling for the classes, it would eventually lift. It's really not that bad most days, just sort of... there in the background. I hope I didn't wait too long to come see you about it. Is nine months... is that typical for this kind of thing?\n\nDoctor: Did something specific happen in your life around the time this sadness started, about nine months ago?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I've racked my brain trying to figure that out, too. I kept thinking, maybe I was just stressed about a recital or maybe I wasn't eating right, but... no. Everything was actually going pretty well. The studio was doing great, my classes were full. \n\nI guess that's part of why I feel a little silly being here. Like, I shouldn't be sad, right? I have so much to be grateful for. I really hope it's okay that I don't have a better reason for feeling this way. Is it... is it normal for it to just kind of happen out of nowhere like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, such as choreographing or teaching dance classes, as much as you did before this sadness started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... It's just that the joy isn't... quite as bright as it used to be? I hope that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since this sadness started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I hope that's not too concerning? I'm sure my appetite will come back soon, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, no, definitely not! I have my boyfriend, and my mom, and my students rely on me. I want to be here for them. Sometimes I might feel a little overwhelmed and just wish I could take a long nap and hide under the covers for a day, but that's just normal stress, right? Everyone feels like that sometimes. I promise I'm not having any scary thoughts or anything like that. I'm doing my best to stay strong. Is that... I mean, that's a good sign, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling better over the past nine months, where the sadness lifted for a while?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's been pretty consistent, actually. I mean, I try really hard to have good days, and sometimes I can put on a smile for my classes and make it through, but... the underlying sadness, that heavy feeling? It's kind of just been there the whole time. \n\nFor about nine months now, I think. I know that sounds like a long time to be feeling this way, and I really have been trying my best to snap out of it, I promise. I don't want to be a burden or seem like I'm not trying to get better. It's just... it's been about nine months since I felt like my normal self. I hope that's okay to admit? I'm sure we can fix it, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your energy levels over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I wouldn't say it's anything too extreme, but I guess I have been feeling a little more tired than usual? I know it's probably just because I'm not moving around as much with my ankle brace and everything. \n\nBut sometimes, um, I just feel kind of wiped out after doing really small things. Like, I'll answer just one email to a dancer about scheduling, or I'll take a shower—which is a whole production with this brace, honestly—and I'll just feel completely exhausted afterward. And even if I go lie down for a bit, it doesn't really seem to recharge me the way it used to. I try not to let it show, and I still get up and do what I need to do! I'm not just lying around all day, I promise. It's just a little harder to find my energy right now. That's pretty normal when you're feeling down, isn't it? I'm sure it's nothing to worry about.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely back to your normal self for at least two months over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... two whole months? I don't... I don't think I've had a stretch quite that long where I felt completely a hundred percent. \n\nI mean, I definitely have good days! I don't want you to think I'm just gloomy all the time. I really try to push through and be upbeat for my dancers, and I do have moments where I feel okay and can just focus on the choreography. But a full two months of feeling totally like my old self... no, I guess that heavy feeling has just sort of lingered underneath it all since this started. \n\nI hope that doesn't mean I'm doing a bad job at managing this? I really am trying my best to get back there. That's what we're working towards, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely back to your normal self for at least eight weeks over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um, eight weeks? That's... that's basically the same as two months, right? From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.1, F33.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm not wasting your time, I know you're probably very busy. But, well, I guess what brings me in is... I've just been waking up lately with this kind of heavy, hollow sadness. \n\nIt's a little embarrassing to admit, honestly, because I usually try to stay so positive for my dancers. But it's almost like... like the studio lights are on, but there’s just no music in me anymore, if that makes sense? I'm a choreographer, so I guess that's just how my brain works. I'm sure it's nothing major, probably just a funk, but I wanted to make sure I'm doing the right thing by getting it checked out. Is that... is that okay to be here for?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this heavy, hollow sadness?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's been a little while. Maybe around nine months? \n\nI know that sounds like a long time to just let it sit, and I really should have come in sooner, I'm sorry. I just kept thinking I could push through it, you know? Like, if I just kept moving and kept smiling for the classes, it would eventually lift. It's really not that bad most days, just sort of... there in the background. I hope I didn't wait too long to come see you about it. Is nine months... is that typical for this kind of thing?\n\nDoctor: Did something specific happen in your life around the time this sadness started, about nine months ago?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I've racked my brain trying to figure that out, too. I kept thinking, maybe I was just stressed about a recital or maybe I wasn't eating right, but... no. Everything was actually going pretty well. The studio was doing great, my classes were full. \n\nI guess that's part of why I feel a little silly being here. Like, I shouldn't be sad, right? I have so much to be grateful for. I really hope it's okay that I don't have a better reason for feeling this way. Is it... is it normal for it to just kind of happen out of nowhere like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, such as choreographing or teaching dance classes, as much as you did before this sadness started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I still enjoy it somewhat. I mean, I love my students, I really do. They're wonderful. But... maybe not quite as much as I used to? \n\nIt's just that it feels a bit more like a chore now, which sounds terrible to say out loud. I feel so guilty even admitting that. I still show up, and I still put on a smile, and I really try to give them my best. I just... I don't get that same spark I used to get when a routine finally comes together. But I'm still doing it! I haven't quit or anything. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a bad teacher. Do a lot of people lose interest in their passions like that?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or things that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's hard to say right now. I mean, I still like going for walks when the weather is nice, and I try to read a little before bed. But... I don't know, nothing really stands out as being super exciting anymore. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably not a very helpful answer. I really wish I could give you a list of things I'm still super passionate about. I just feel so flat, you know? But I am trying! I promise I'm trying to stay active and do things. Is it... is it okay that I can't really think of anything right now?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you normally find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little bit? Like I mentioned with the dancing, it's just... it's just not quite the same. But I wouldn't say I've completely lost interest in *everything*. \n\nI mean, I still watch movies with my boyfriend sometimes, and I'll still call my mom on Sundays. I really do try to keep up with my normal life. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing all day. I'm definitely still trying to participate in things. It's just that the joy isn't... quite as bright as it used to be? I hope that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since this sadness started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe I've lost a little bit of weight? Nothing too crazy, I promise! I mean, when I was feeling really energetic a while back and buzzing with all those choreography ideas, I would just sort of... skip meals without even noticing because I was so focused. \n\nAnd now, well... honestly, food just kind of tastes like cardboard lately. I still try to eat, I really do! I know it's important to keep my strength up for my students. But my rehearsal clothes have definitely been hanging a bit looser than usual. I hope that's not too concerning? I'm sure my appetite will come back soon, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, no, definitely not! I mean, I know things have been a little tough lately, but I would never... I don't think like that. \n\nI really try to focus on the positive things, you know? I have my boyfriend, and my mom, and my students rely on me. I want to be here for them. Sometimes I might feel a little overwhelmed and just wish I could take a long nap and hide under the covers for a day, but that's just normal stress, right? Everyone feels like that sometimes. I promise I'm not having any scary thoughts or anything like that. I'm doing my best to stay strong. Is that... I mean, that's a good sign, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling better over the past nine months, where the sadness lifted for a while?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's been pretty consistent, actually. I mean, I try really hard to have good days, and sometimes I can put on a smile for my classes and make it through, but... the underlying sadness, that heavy feeling? It's kind of just been there the whole time. \n\nFor about nine months now, I think. I know that sounds like a long time to be feeling this way, and I really have been trying my best to snap out of it, I promise. I don't want to be a burden or seem like I'm not trying to get better. It's just... it's been about nine months since I felt like my normal self. I hope that's okay to admit? I'm sure we can fix it, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your energy levels over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I wouldn't say it's anything too extreme, but I guess I have been feeling a little more tired than usual? I know it's probably just because I'm not moving around as much with my ankle brace and everything. \n\nBut sometimes, um, I just feel kind of wiped out after doing really small things. Like, I'll answer just one email to a dancer about scheduling, or I'll take a shower—which is a whole production with this brace, honestly—and I'll just feel completely exhausted afterward. And even if I go lie down for a bit, it doesn't really seem to recharge me the way it used to. I try not to let it show, and I still get up and do what I need to do! I'm not just lying around all day, I promise. It's just a little harder to find my energy right now. That's pretty normal when you're feeling down, isn't it? I'm sure it's nothing to worry about.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely back to your normal self for at least two months over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... two whole months? I don't... I don't think I've had a stretch quite that long where I felt completely a hundred percent. \n\nI mean, I definitely have good days! I don't want you to think I'm just gloomy all the time. I really try to push through and be upbeat for my dancers, and I do have moments where I feel okay and can just focus on the choreography. But a full two months of feeling totally like my old self... no, I guess that heavy feeling has just sort of lingered underneath it all since this started. \n\nI hope that doesn't mean I'm doing a bad job at managing this? I really am trying my best to get back there. That's what we're working towards, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely back to your normal self for at least eight weeks over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um, eight weeks? That's... that's basically the same as two months, right? So, yeah, I guess my answer is still the same as what I just said. \n\nI really wish I could say yes, because I know that's what you want to hear, and I want to be making progress for you. But honestly, no. The... the sad part, that heavy feeling I mentioned? It's just sort of been hanging around this whole time, for the whole nine months. I haven't had a clear stretch of eight weeks where it completely went away. I'm sorry if that's disappointing. I really am trying to snap out of it, I swear. Is there something else I should be doing to make those good periods last longer?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this heavy sadness for most of every day over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I have. I really hate admitting that, because I feel like I should be stronger, you know? I want to be doing better for you. But... almost every single day, I've been waking up with this... this heavy, hollow sadness. \n\nIt’s sort of like... well, like the studio lights are on, but there’s just no music in me anymore. If that makes sense? I don't want to sound overly dramatic or anything, I really don't. I still show up and smile for my classes, and I try so hard to keep a brave face! But that hollow feeling is just kind of sitting there for most of the day, no matter how hard I try to shake it off. \n\nI hope I'm explaining that right... is that a helpful way to describe it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of at least two weeks where your sadness was significantly worse than it has been over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um... significantly worse for two weeks? I don't... I don't think so. I mean, it's been pretty steady, that heavy feeling I was talking about. \n\nI haven't really had a specific time where it just completely nose-dived and stayed there for weeks on end. I try really hard not to let it get to that point, you know? I always try to pull myself back up before it gets too bad. So, no, I don't think I've had a two-week stretch where it was drastically worse than the rest of the time. \n\nI hope that's a good sign? Like, maybe that means I'm at least keeping it somewhat manageable? I just really want to make sure I'm on the right track here.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um... sleep? I guess it's been a little bit uneven, maybe? I really hope that's normal. I mean, I try to get my eight hours, I really do, because I know how important rest is for my body. \n\nBut... well, it's been a little all over the place. Like, there were those times when I just had so much energy, and I'd stay up sketching out new choreography formations until dawn. I felt so productive then, so I hope that's okay? But lately... lately it's different. I'll fall asleep because I'm just so exhausted, but then I keep waking up really early, like around four or five in the morning, and my stomach just feels... tight. Like I'm anxious, but I don't know why. \n\nI try to go back to sleep, I promise I do. I just lie there and try to relax. Is it bad that my sleep is so messed up? I really want to fix it if I'm doing something wrong.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I still love dance. I really do. It's my whole life, you know? I try so hard to stay engaged with my students and the choreography. \n\nI guess... well, maybe I don't get that same rush of excitement when I put a new routine together right now, but I still do it! I still show up and put in the work. I don't want you to think I'm just giving up on the things I care about. I'm really trying to keep up with everything. \n\nSo, I wouldn't say I don't find pleasure in them anymore... just maybe it takes a little more effort to find it? I hope that makes sense. Is it normal to have to work a little harder to enjoy things sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two months where you didn't feel this heavy sadness?\n\nPatient: Um... two months without it? I don't... I don't think so. Not recently, anyway. \n\nI mean, I've been trying really hard to stay positive, but this... this heavy feeling, it's kind of been hanging around for, gosh, maybe about nine months now? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not trying to get better. I really am! I just... it's been pretty consistent since then. \n\nI haven't really had a solid two-month break from it where I felt completely back to normal. Is that... is that okay to admit? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so we can figure this out together.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in activities that normally bring you joy?\n\nPatient: Um... I think we might have just talked about that a little bit? About the dance and the choreography? I hope it's okay that I brought it up before you asked this specific question. \n\nLike I said, I wouldn't say I've lost pleasure in it completely. I'm really trying my best to stay involved and keep doing what I love. It's just... maybe a little less exciting right now, but I'm still doing it! I haven't stopped doing the things that normally bring me joy. I promise I'm still trying. Is that what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm answering it right.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks where you felt completely depressed, with either a depressed mood or loss of interest in activities, and at least several other symptoms like changes in appetite or sleep?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I think so. I mean, like I mentioned a minute ago, this... this down feeling has been with me for about nine months now. \n\nBut I don't want you to think I've just been lying in bed doing nothing that whole time! I've been trying so hard to keep my routine, keep eating right, and, you know, pushing through it. I know it's been a long time, but I really have been doing my best to manage it. \n\nSo, yes, it's definitely been longer than two weeks... but I'm still functioning, I promise! Does that help answer your question? I hope I'm giving you what you need.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
